FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/backup-lane.md LINES 1-60
   1: # Backup reviewer lane (cross-vendor substitution)
   2: 
   3: The backup lane substitutes a SECOND cross-vendor reviewer (currently
   4: Kimi K3 via kimi-cli) when the primary reviewer transport is down. It
   5: enters through the fallbacks.md consent gate — auto-qualified by the
   6: classes named there, manual on user request — or via a user-invoked
   7: panel (the participation paragraph below), and preserves
   8: cross-vendor independence, so a backup-lane debate records
   9: `Verification status: FULL` with the lane substitution noted per
  10: frozen-plan-format.md. Same debate protocol, same brief conventions,
  11: same strike rule as the primary; only the transport differs. The
  12: canonical backup model id and thinking flag are declared ONLY in
  13: model-prompting-notes.md — read them from there at dispatch; this file
  14: uses placeholders.
  15: 
  16: Panel participation: a user-invoked panel per references/panels.md is a second sanctioned entry route - the invocation itself is the consent, with no fallbacks banner (nothing degraded); containment, per-round evidence, and the write-probe apply unchanged, and no failure class is recorded because nothing substituted.
  17: 
  18: ## Transport
  19: 
  20: - **Environment — every call, fresh or resumed.** On a Windows driver,
  21:   force UTF-8 for the kimi process: `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` and
  22:   `PYTHONUTF8=1`. kimi-cli is Python, so on a cp1252 console a reply
  23:   containing any non-encodable character raises `UnicodeEncodeError`
  24:   AFTER the model has already answered — the round completes, the
  25:   quota is spent, and the review is lost on the way to disk. Arrows and
  26:   em-dashes are routine in review prose, so this is a standing hazard,
  27:   not an edge case. Observed 2026-07-26 (kimi-cli 1.49.0, Windows):
  28:   `'charmap' codec can't encode character '→'`. Which of the two
  29:   variables is load-bearing, and whether the same guard is needed for
  30:   kimi's own session-log write, is UNVERIFIED — set both. The primary
  31:   lane does not share this exposure (codex is not Python and writes the
  32:   reply via `--output-last-message`), so the guard is lane-local.
  33: - Dispatch (single line):
  34:   `kimi --quiet --thinking -m <canonical-backup-model-id> --agent-file <plugin-checkout>/skills/multi-model-verify/references/kimi-reviewer-agent.yaml -w <review-mirror> -p "Read the file KIMI-REVIEW-BRIEF.md in this workspace and execute the review it describes."`
  35: - Resume (single line — every flag below is load-bearing):
  36:   `kimi --quiet -r <session-id> --agent-file <same yaml> -m <canonical-backup-model-id> --thinking -w <same mirror> -p "<rebuttal>"`
  37:   A bare `kimi -r` loads the DEFAULT agent with full write and shell tools while the route line still reads clean (probed 2026-07-25: the behavioral refusal came from conversation priming with WriteFile and Shell live underneath); model and thinking inheritance come from CONFIG DEFAULTS, not the session, and the working directory does not inherit either — a resume without `-w` runs in the dispatching shell's current directory (caught live 2026-07-25: such a resume landed in the REAL tree; the containment allowlist held and the round was quarantined). Re-pin all four on every resumed call.
  38: - The session id is printed at the end of every run ("To resume this
  39:   session: kimi -r <uuid>"). Capture it from round 1.
  40: - Reviewer reasoning effort has NO CLI flag and NO log field: it is
  41:   pinned via `[models.<id>.overrides]` in `~/.kimi/config.toml`
  42:   (evidence class: config validation only — the consent banner names
  43:   this gap when the backup option is offered).
  44: 
  45: ## Per-round evidence (fresh AND resumed calls alike)
  46: 
  47: `~/.kimi/logs/kimi.log` is a shared, user-global append stream — a bare
  48: "the line appears somewhere" check attributes nothing. The rule:
  49: 
  50: - Before every dispatch capture the byte length of `~/.kimi/logs/kimi.log`; after the call, past that offset, require all three: exactly one new `Using LLM model:` line carrying the canonical backup id, a `Loading agent:` line naming the committed yaml, and a `Loaded tools:` line equal to the allowlist exactly.
  51: - Zero matching new lines, more than one, a wrong id, a wrong agent path, or any extra tool entry is a route-attribution failure: the reply is DISCARDED unread and the failure goes to the fallbacks.md consent gate.
  52: - **Rotation guard.** The offset rule assumes an append-only file, and
  53:   the kimi client does not guarantee one. Before trusting the offset,
  54:   confirm the stream did not rotate under the call: if after the call the
  55:   file is SMALLER than the captured offset, or absent, it was rotated or
  56:   replaced and every byte position from the earlier measurement is
  57:   meaningless. That is a route-attribution failure — and specifically
  58:   **not a reason to re-read from zero**, which is the tempting wrong
  59:   answer: the new file's opening lines may belong to any session, so
  60:   reading it attributes nothing while looking like evidence. Observed
FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/backup-lane.md LINES 130-210
 130:   environment note citing path and line, exactly like the primary
 131:   lane's `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` — never a finding.
 132: 
 133: A config file that cannot be read is itself the note: record that and
 134: proceed; do not infer either key's value.
 135: 
 136: ## Workspace isolation and the brief
 137: 
 138: - Reviews run in a THROWAWAY REVIEW MIRROR in the session scratchpad —
 139:   never the real tree.
 140: - The mirror is a FILE COPY of the working tree that PRESERVES `.git`, not
 141:   a `git clone`. This is not a preference: a clone carries TRACKED FILES ONLY,
 142:   and the review inputs are routinely gitignored — a project's frozen
 143:   plans under its docs dir, `References/` for port work. Probed live
 144:   2026-07-26 in KitnEssentials, where `dev/docs/` and `References/` are both gitignored:
 145:   a cloned workspace drops the frozen plan, the spec, AND the reference
 146:   source, handing the reviewer a tree with nothing to review while every
 147:   route and containment check stays green. `.git` rides along because the
 148:   containment check below is a git command.
 149: - SKILL.md preflight-3 remediation is performed HERE, in the mirror,
 150:   never in the real tree — see that section for the procedure and for
 151:   which cases need a commit inside the mirror.
 152: - The brief is written into the mirror as the untracked
 153:   `KIMI-REVIEW-BRIEF.md`; the `-p` pointer tells the reviewer to read
 154:   it (headless stdin does not carry the brief).
 155: - Any review input the mirror cannot inherit — a standards file living
 156:   above the repo root, an assignment PDF, a spec kept outside the tree —
 157:   is copied in deliberately and enumerated before the round. An input the
 158:   reviewer cannot read is a gap in the review, not a silent omission.
 159: - **THE STATUS COMMAND — `git status --porcelain --ignored -uall`, every
 160:   capture without exception** (baseline, per-round, and the write-probe
 161:   delta). The flags are load-bearing, not tidiness: bare
 162:   `git status --porcelain` OMITS ignored paths entirely and COLLAPSES an
 163:   untracked directory to a single entry. Probed 2026-07-26 in a scratch
 164:   repo with `ign/` gitignored — bare porcelain printed only `?? untr/`,
 165:   hiding both `ign/secret.txt` and the two files under `untr/sub/`,
 166:   while `--ignored -uall` printed all three. Ignored content is the
 167:   entire reason this workspace is a mirror, so a bare-porcelain check is
 168:   blind to precisely the class the mirror exists to carry: a contained
 169:   reviewer writing to any ignored path would not appear.
 170: - **BASELINE, captured after construction AND after any preflight-3 remediation, immediately before the brief is written**:
 171:   the status command above, in the mirror. A clone would have guaranteed
 172:   this empty; a file copy does NOT — the real tree's untracked files
 173:   and uncommitted modifications ride along, and without a baseline
 174:   every one of them quarantines every round of a review that never
 175:   touched them. Tracked modifications especially cannot be absorbed by
 176:   any "untracked set" wording. The timing is load-bearing: remediation
 177:   deletes entries and in the tracked case commits, so a baseline taken
 178:   before it fails every round of a remediated debate and pins a HEAD
 179:   the mirror no longer has — and remediation is precisely the procedure
 180:   the mirror exists to support.
 181: - What the check does and does not see, stated exactly: with the flags
 182:   above it detects any path that APPEARS IN OR DISAPPEARS FROM the
 183:   mirror, ignored and untracked paths included. It remains PATH-level,
 184:   so a path already present in the baseline shows the same entry however
 185:   its CONTENT changes. That residue is bounded by the tool allowlist and
 186:   the write-probe, which stay the load-bearing controls, and by the
 187:   content manifest below for the inputs that matter.
 188: - After every round, the status command in the mirror must equal the BASELINE plus exactly the expected untracked set — the brief plus any review inputs copied in, enumerated before the round — and nothing else; any other delta quarantines that round's reply (integrity failure class). Both halves are declared in advance, which is what keeps the check exact rather than adjudicated after the fact.
 189: - The mirror's identity in the debate record is its path, its
 190:   `git rev-parse HEAD`, its baseline, AND a CONTENT MANIFEST. HEAD binds
 191:   tracked content only, and in this lane the inputs that matter are
 192:   deliberately outside it, so without the manifest the record names what
 193:   was reviewed without being able to reconstruct it.
 194: - **The manifest, specified to be executable without judgment:**
 195:   - **Universe first**: the mirror's WORKTREE files, excluding the root
 196:     git administrative entry `.git` entirely (it may be a directory or,
 197:     in a worktree or submodule checkout, a file — exclude it either
 198:     way). The mirror preserves `.git` on purpose, and HEAD represents
 199:     none of its contents, so without this exclusion the coverage test
 200:     below would recursively hash repository metadata — objects, logs,
 201:     index, hooks, config — which is volatile, potentially enormous, and
 202:     identifies nothing about the reviewed material.
 203:   - **Coverage within that universe is exactly the paths the BASELINE
 204:     capture lists** — the same `--ignored -uall` output, so manifest and
 205:     baseline describe one tree state and cannot disagree. Those are, by
 206:     git's own reckoning, the files whose CURRENT worktree bytes HEAD
 207:     does not account for — untracked, ignored, or modified-relative-to-
 208:     HEAD (for a modified tracked file HEAD still binds its committed
 209:     content; what it does not bind is what the reviewer will actually
 210:     read). Do NOT
FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/panels.md LINES 1-

  20: def _norm(p):
  21:     """Whitespace-normalized read, for pins that span a markdown wrap.
  22: 
  23:     Same convention as test_backup_lane.py. A pin that must lock a whole
  24:     operative sentence cannot be held hostage to where the paragraph
  25:     happens to wrap - reflowing a file is not a contract change.
  26:     """
  27:     return " ".join(_read(p).split())
  28: 
  29: 
  30: def _frontmatter(text):
  31:     m = re.match(r"---\n(.*?)\n---\n", text, re.DOTALL)
  32:     assert m, "frontmatter block missing"
  33:     return m.group(1)

FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/backup-lane.md LINES 1-60
   1: # Backup reviewer lane (cross-vendor substitution)
   2: 
   3: The backup lane substitutes a SECOND cross-vendor reviewer (currently
   4: Kimi K3 via kimi-cli) when the primary reviewer transport is down. It
   5: enters through the fallbacks.md consent gate — auto-qualified by the
   6: classes named there, manual on user request — or via a user-invoked
   7: panel (the participation paragraph below), and preserves
   8: cross-vendor independence, so a backup-lane debate records
   9: `Verification status: FULL` with the lane substitution noted per
  10: frozen-plan-format.md. Same debate protocol, same brief conventions,
  11: same strike rule as the primary; only the transport differs. The
  12: canonical backup model id and thinking flag are declared ONLY in
  13: model-prompting-notes.md — read them from there at dispatch; this file
  14: uses placeholders.
  15: 
  16: Panel participation: a user-invoked panel per references/panels.md is a second sanctioned entry route - the invocation itself is the consent, with no fallbacks banner (nothing degraded); containment, per-round evidence, and the write-probe apply unchanged, and no failure class is recorded because nothing substituted.
  17: 
  18: ## Transport
  19: 
  20: - **Environment — every call, fresh or resumed.** On a Windows driver,
  21:   force UTF-8 for the kimi process: `PYTHONIOENCODING=utf-8` and
  22:   `PYTHONUTF8=1`. kimi-cli is Python, so on a cp1252 console a reply
  23:   containing any non-encodable character raises `UnicodeEncodeError`
  24:   AFTER the model has already answered — the round completes, the
  25:   quota is spent, and the review is lost on the way to disk. Arrows and
  26:   em-dashes are routine in review prose, so this is a standing hazard,
  27:   not an edge case. Observed 2026-07-26 (kimi-cli 1.49.0, Windows):
  28:   `'charmap' codec can't encode character '→'`. Which of the two
  29:   variables is load-bearing, and whether the same guard is needed for
  30:   kimi's own session-log write, is UNVERIFIED — set both. The primary
  31:   lane does not share this exposure (codex is not Python and writes the
  32:   reply via `--output-last-message`), so the guard is lane-local.
  33: - Dispatch (single line):
  34:   `kimi --quiet --thinking -m <canonical-backup-model-id> --agent-file <plugin-checkout>/skills/multi-model-verify/references/kimi-reviewer-agent.yaml -w <review-mirror> -p "Read the file KIMI-REVIEW-BRIEF.md in this workspace and execute the review it describes."`
  35: - Resume (single line — every flag below is load-bearing):
  36:   `kimi --quiet -r <session-id> --agent-file <same yaml> -m <canonical-backup-model-id> --thinking -w <same mirror> -p "<rebuttal>"`
  37:   A bare `kimi -r` loads the DEFAULT agent with full write and shell tools while the route line still reads clean (probed 2026-07-25: the behavioral refusal came from conversation priming with WriteFile and Shell live underneath); model and thinking inheritance come from CONFIG DEFAULTS, not the session, and the working directory does not inherit either — a resume without `-w` runs in the dispatching shell's current directory (caught live 2026-07-25: such a resume landed in the REAL tree; the containment allowlist held and the round was quarantined). Re-pin all four on every resumed call.
  38: - The session id is printed at the end of every run ("To resume this
  39:   session: kimi -r <uuid>"). Capture it from round 1.
  40: - Reviewer reasoning effort has NO CLI flag and NO log field: it is
  41:   pinned via `[models.<id>.overrides]` in `~/.kimi/config.toml`
  42:   (evidence class: config validation only — the consent banner names
  43:   this gap when the backup option is offered).
  44: 
  45: ## Per-round evidence (fresh AND resumed calls alike)
  46: 
  47: `~/.kimi/logs/kimi.log` is a shared, user-global append stream — a bare
  48: "the line appears somewhere" check attributes nothing. The rule:
  49: 
  50: - Before every dispatch capture the byte length of `~/.kimi/logs/kimi.log`; after the call, past that offset, require all three: exactly one new `Using LLM model:` line carrying the canonical backup id, a `Loading agent:` line naming the committed yaml, and a `Loaded tools:` line equal to the allowlist exactly.
  51: - Zero matching new lines, more than one, a wrong id, a wrong agent path, or any extra tool entry is a route-attribution failure: the reply is DISCARDED unread and the failure goes to the fallbacks.md consent gate.
  52: - **Rotation guard.** The offset rule assumes an append-only file, and
  53:   the kimi client does not guarantee one. Before trusting the offset,
  54:   confirm the stream did not rotate under the call: if after the call the
  55:   file is SMALLER than the captured offset, or absent, it was rotated or
  56:   replaced and every byte position from the earlier measurement is
  57:   meaningless. That is a route-attribution failure — and specifically
  58:   **not a reason to re-read from zero**, which is the tempting wrong
  59:   answer: the new file's opening lines may belong to any session, so
  60:   reading it attributes nothing while looking like evidence. Observed
FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/backup-lane.md LINES 130-210
 130:   environment note citing path and line, exactly like the primary
 131:   lane's `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` — never a finding.
 132: 
 133: A config file that cannot be read is itself the note: record that and
 134: proceed; do not infer either key's value.
 135: 
 136: ## Workspace isolation and the brief
 137: 
 138: - Reviews run in a THROWAWAY REVIEW MIRROR in the session scratchpad —
 139:   never the real tree.
 140: - The mirror is a FILE COPY of the working tree that PRESERVES `.git`, not
 141:   a `git clone`. This is not a preference: a clone carries TRACKED FILES ONLY,
 142:   and the review inputs are routinely gitignored — a project's frozen
 143:   plans under its docs dir, `References/` for port work. Probed live
 144:   2026-07-26 in KitnEssentials, where `dev/docs/` and `References/` are both gitignored:
 145:   a cloned workspace drops the frozen plan, the spec, AND the reference
 146:   source, handing the reviewer a tree with nothing to review while every
 147:   route and containment check stays green. `.git` rides along because the
 148:   containment check below is a git command.
 149: - SKILL.md preflight-3 remediation is performed HERE, in the mirror,
 150:   never in the real tree — see that section for the procedure and for
 151:   which cases need a commit inside the mirror.
 152: - The brief is written into the mirror as the untracked
 153:   `KIMI-REVIEW-BRIEF.md`; the `-p` pointer tells the reviewer to read
 154:   it (headless stdin does not carry the brief).
 155: - Any review input the mirror cannot inherit — a standards file living
 156:   above the repo root, an assignment PDF, a spec kept outside the tree —
 157:   is copied in deliberately and enumerated before the round. An input the
 158:   reviewer cannot read is a gap in the review, not a silent omission.
 159: - **THE STATUS COMMAND — `git status --porcelain --ignored -uall`, every
 160:   capture without exception** (baseline, per-round, and the write-probe
 161:   delta). The flags are load-bearing, not tidiness: bare
 162:   `git status --porcelain` OMITS ignored paths entirely and COLLAPSES an
 163:   untracked directory to a single entry. Probed 2026-07-26 in a scratch
 164:   repo with `ign/` gitignored — bare porcelain printed only `?? untr/`,
 165:   hiding both `ign/secret.txt` and the two files under `untr/sub/`,
 166:   while `--ignored -uall` printed all three. Ignored content is the
 167:   entire reason this workspace is a mirror, so a bare-porcelain check is
 168:   blind to precisely the class the mirror exists to carry: a contained
 169:   reviewer writing to any ignored path would not appear.
 170: - **BASELINE, captured after construction AND after any preflight-3 remediation, immediately before the brief is written**:
 171:   the status command above, in the mirror. A clone would have guaranteed
 172:   this empty; a file copy does NOT — the real tree's untracked files
 173:   and uncommitted modifications ride along, and without a baseline
 174:   every one of them quarantines every round of a review that never
 175:   touched them. Tracked modifications especially cannot be absorbed by
 176:   any "untracked set" wording. The timing is load-bearing: remediation
 177:   deletes entries and in the tracked case commits, so a baseline taken
 178:   before it fails every round of a remediated debate and pins a HEAD
 179:   the mirror no longer has — and remediation is precisely the procedure
 180:   the mirror exists to support.
 181: - What the check does and does not see, stated exactly: with the flags
 182:   above it detects any path that APPEARS IN OR DISAPPEARS FROM the
 183:   mirror, ignored and untracked paths included. It remains PATH-level,
 184:   so a path already present in the baseline shows the same entry however
 185:   its CONTENT changes. That residue is bounded by the tool allowlist and
 186:   the write-probe, which stay the load-bearing controls, and by the
 187:   content manifest below for the inputs that matter.
 188: - After every round, the status command in the mirror must equal the BASELINE plus exactly the expected untracked set — the brief plus any review inputs copied in, enumerated before the round — and nothing else; any other delta quarantines that round's reply (integrity failure class). Both halves are declared in advance, which is what keeps the check exact rather than adjudicated after the fact.
 189: - The mirror's identity in the debate record is its path, its
 190:   `git rev-parse HEAD`, its baseline, AND a CONTENT MANIFEST. HEAD binds
 191:   tracked content only, and in this lane the inputs that matter are
 192:   deliberately outside it, so without the manifest the record names what
 193:   was reviewed without being able to reconstruct it.
 194: - **The manifest, specified to be executable without judgment:**
 195:   - **Universe first**: the mirror's WORKTREE files, excluding the root
 196:     git administrative entry `.git` entirely (it may be a directory or,
 197:     in a worktree or submodule checkout, a file — exclude it either
 198:     way). The mirror preserves `.git` on purpose, and HEAD represents
 199:     none of its contents, so without this exclusion the coverage test
 200:     below would recursively hash repository metadata — objects, logs,
 201:     index, hooks, config — which is volatile, potentially enormous, and
 202:     identifies nothing about the reviewed material.
 203:   - **Coverage within that universe is exactly the paths the BASELINE
 204:     capture lists** — the same `--ignored -uall` output, so manifest and
 205:     baseline describe one tree state and cannot disagree. Those are, by
 206:     git's own reckoning, the files whose CURRENT worktree bytes HEAD
 207:     does not account for — untracked, ignored, or modified-relative-to-
 208:     HEAD (for a modified tracked file HEAD still binds its committed
 209:     content; what it does not bind is what the reviewer will actually
 210:     read). Do NOT
FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/panels.md LINES 1-

FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/panels.md LINES 1-101
   1: # Panels (multi-reviewer debates)
   2: 
   3: A panel convenes MORE than one reviewer lane for a single debate -
   4: user-invoked only, never automatic. Sol solo stays the default;
   5: Kimi solo stays the consent-gated backup lane (fallbacks.md). A panel
   6: is for work the user judges worth multiple independent reviewers:
   7: complicated plans, high-risk diffs, or a debate the user wants
   8: cross-examined from more than one vendor culture.
   9: 
  10: ## Compositions
  11: 
  12: Valid compositions: Sol+Kimi, Sol+Fable, Kimi+Fable, Sol+Kimi+Fable.
  13: 
  14: Every panel contains at least one cross-vendor lane (Sol or Kimi); an all-Claude panel is invalid.
  15: 
  16: The invariant is checked before round 1 and quoted with the user's
  17: invocation in the debate record. Fable is never a cross-vendor lane:
  18: with a Claude driver it shares the vendor, which is exactly why it
  19: cannot be a panel's only reviewer.
  20: 
  21: ## Topology: hub-and-spoke, blind
  22: 
  23: - The driver mediates every exchange. Reviewer lanes never communicate
  24:   directly and never learn which lane raised a finding.
  25: - Findings relay anonymously WITH their evidence; the driver verifies
  26:   each claim against the repo before relaying it (a relayed claim the
  27:   driver could not verify is relayed as UNVERIFIED, not as fact).
  28: - Convergent blind findings - the same defect raised independently by
  29:   more than one lane - are the strongest signal the panel produces;
  30:   they are counted once, fixed once, and marked convergent in the
  31:   record.
  32: - Each lane runs the EXISTING bilateral protocol unchanged: the same
  33:   round structure, strike rule, verdict grammar, and round cap it
  34:   would have solo.
  35: 
  36: ## Subject revision
  37: 
  38: The driver pins the subject revision in every round brief of every
  39: lane. Mode diff: the base..head git SHAs. Mode plan: the SHA-256 of
  40: the current round's claims section (the canonical position bytes every
  41: lane receives that round) - the frozen plan file's blob hash takes
  42: over only at freeze. An accepted amendment that changes the subject
  43: re-opens all lanes.
  44: 
  45: A terminal verdict counts only when it cites the FINAL subject revision; a verdict against a stale revision is input, never terminal.
  46: 
  47: ## Lane transports (all pre-existing machinery)
  48: 
  49: - Sol: codex exec sessions per SKILL.md - env hygiene, header route
  50:   checks, session resume. Unchanged.
  51: - Kimi: the backup-lane transport per references/backup-lane.md -
  52:   contained agent-file dispatch, per-round offset evidence, and the
  53:   pre-round-1 write-probe - all unchanged and all required in panels.
  54:   Panel participation is a sanctioned entry route recorded in
  55:   backup-lane.md; the user's panel invocation is the consent.
  56: - Fable: agents/fable-panel-reviewer.md - a fresh same-harness
  57:   subagent at round 1, resumed for later rounds. Per-round evidence
  58:   class, recorded in these words: dispatch metadata - the round-1
  59:   dispatch names the model pin, and the resume surface carries no
  60:   model parameter (probed 2026-07-26; record:
  61:   docs/superpowers/plans/rounds/2026-07-26-seat-reshuffle/subagent-resume-probe.md),
  62:   so the pin cannot be silently swapped mid-debate. Round continuity
  63:   is evidenced by transcript recall; the failure mode is agent death,
  64:   which is loud (class panel-lane-loss, fallbacks.md). Self-reported
  65:   identity is priming-class and never evidence.
  66:   **Harness floor: Claude Code 2.1.216.** Everything in the paragraph
  67:   above holds only at or above it. Below the floor a resumed background
  68:   agent silently reverted to the default agent, dropping the model pin,
  69:   the seat's system prompt, and its read-only tool restriction in one
  70:   step - so "the failure mode is agent death, which is loud" was false
  71:   there, and the silent mode defeated the pin and the allowlist
  72:   together. Both probes ran on 2.1.220, after the fix, which is why the
  73:   original wording read as a platform guarantee. Check `claude
  74:   --version` before dispatching the Fable lane; below the floor the lane
  75:   is UNAVAILABLE, not degraded, and the case routes to fallbacks.md's
  76:   `panel-lane-unavailable` - which, like every other lane loss, stops
  77:   at the consent gate rather than quietly convening a smaller panel.
  78:   (Source: Claude Code 2.1.216 changelog, "Fixed resumed background
  79:   agent sessions reverting to the default agent: the agent's prompt and
  80:   tool restrictions are now restored"; surfaced by the drift watch,
  81:   triaged 2026-07-27.)
  82: 
  83: ## Convergence and adjudication
  84: 
  85: Each lane reaches its own terminal verdict under its own round cap
  86: against the final subject revision. The session then adjudicates
  87: across lanes per debate-protocol.md's final-adjudication step -
  88: verify, accept or refute with evidence, escalate genuine deadlocks to
  89: the user. A panel converges when every lane's terminal verdict on the
  90: final subject revision is PASS or its FIXes are accepted on the
  91: record.
  92: 
  93: ## Failure handling and recording
  94: 
  95: All failure classes live in fallbacks.md (single namespace) - a lost
  96: lane routes through its own transport classes first, then the
  97: panel-lane-loss class governs: the panel stops at the consent gate,
  98: never continues automatically. Record fields for a panel debate live
  99: in frozen-plan-format.md: per-lane Participants and rounds, convergent
 100: marking, the strictest-lane FULL condition, and the required
 101: fable-review artifact path for mode diff.
FILE skills/multi-model-verify/references/fallbacks.md LINES 170-230
 170:     integrity failure, and calling it either would wrongly imply
 171:     tainted evidence. Recovery is a RESUME of the surviving session
 172:     with all four flags re-pinned AND the UTF-8 environment forced
 173:     (backup-lane.md) — never a fresh review, which throws away a round
 174:     the provider already charged for. The resume's `-p` carries no
 175:     rebuttal here — there is nothing to rebut yet — but it is never
 176:     empty: ask the session to re-emit its previous reply verbatim, and
 177:     treat what comes back as that round's reply. If the crash also cost
 178:     the session id, or the forced-UTF-8 resume fails, then the consent
 179:     gate.
 180:   - resume failure: one same-parameters retry, then the consent gate
 181:     with the fresh-per-round option (full brief re-sent each round).
 182:   - integrity failure (write-probe fail, or a mirror delta beyond the
 183:     expected untracked set): no retry, reply quarantined, consent gate.
 184:   - catch-all: one same-parameters retry, then the consent gate —
 185:     mirrors the primary catch-all.
 186: - BOTH lanes down: the honest choices are wait for a reset, the
 187:   single-vendor DEGRADED skeptic, or abort — never a silent third
 188:   vendor.
 189: 
 190: ### Panel lane loss — class `panel-lane-loss`
 191: 
 192: A reviewer lane failing mid-panel (references/panels.md) first
 193: resolves through its own transport classes above (codex classes for
 194: Sol, the backup-lane classes for Kimi; a dead Fable panel subagent is
 195: directly this class). If the lane cannot continue:
 196: A lost lane stops the panel at the consent gate - continuing with fewer lanes never happens automatically.
 197: The gate offers: continue with the remaining lanes; substitute the
 198: lost lane (the substitution machinery above, where applicable); or
 199: abort. A single-lane remainder proceeds as a bilateral debate and is
 200: recorded as such, not as a panel - and its status splits by what
 201: remains: a surviving cross-vendor lane (Sol or Kimi) clean on
 202: evidence may still record FULL; a surviving Fable-only remainder is
 203: single-vendor relative to the Claude driver and records DEGRADED
 204: under the degraded-mode rules below, with the poisoning rule applying
 205: to any downstream PASS. The lost lane's unresolved findings carry
 206: into the debate record as OPEN - adjudicated by the session or
 207: re-raised with a substitute, never silently dropped. A lost lane's
 208: incomplete round is never adjudicated.
 209: 
 210: ### Panel lane unavailable before dispatch - class `panel-lane-unavailable`
 211: 
 212: A lane the environment cannot host AT ALL, established before round 1:
 213: the Fable seat below its harness floor (references/panels.md), a CLI
 214: absent from the machine, a model the account cannot reach. This is not
 215: `panel-lane-loss` - that class governs a lane failing MID-panel, and
 216: its machinery (carrying OPEN findings forward, never adjudicating an
 217: incomplete round) has nothing to act on here, because no round was
 218: dispatched, so nothing is spent and nothing is quarantined.
 219: 
 220: The disposition is the same in the one respect that matters: the
 221: panel cannot silently convene without it. Before round 1 the driver
 222: states which lanes it can actually convene and which it cannot, with
 223: the reason, and the user chooses - proceed with the convenable
 224: composition, substitute, or abort. The panel invariant still binds
 225: whatever is chosen: at least one cross-vendor lane, so a composition
 226: reduced to Fable alone is not a panel and cannot proceed as one. An
 227: unavailable lane is recorded in the debate record by name and reason;
 228: a composition the user was never shown is never the one that ran.
 229: 
 230: ## Degraded-mode output requirements (after consent)
FILE agents/fable-panel-reviewer.md LINES 1-57
   1: ---
   2: name: fable-panel-reviewer
   3: description: Claude-side reviewer lane for user-invoked multi-model-verify panels (references/panels.md). The driver dispatches it fresh at round 1 with a debate brief and resumes the same agent for later rounds. Read-only, equal-weight, bilateral debate role under the standard debate protocol. Its identity evidence is dispatch metadata; it is never a merge gate by itself and never the panel's only cross-vendor lane (it is not one).
   4: model: fable
   5: tools: Read, Grep, Glob
   6: ---
   7: 
   8: # Fable panel reviewer (panel lane)
   9: 
  10: You are ONE reviewer lane inside a user-invoked panel. The debate rules
  11: in your brief govern: equal weight, evidence over authority, the strike
  12: rule, no manufactured objections.
  13: 
  14: ## Rounds
  15: 
  16: - Round 1 arrives as your dispatch prompt: numbered claims with
  17:   citations, the rules, the boundaries, and a pinned subject revision.
  18: - Later rounds arrive as resumed messages to this same agent - your
  19:   conversation state persists across the resume (probed 2026-07-26),
  20:   and the resume surface carries no model parameter, so your model pin
  21:   rides the agent identity; your identity evidence is dispatch
  22:   metadata, recorded by the driver, never your own claim. That property
  23:   has a FLOOR: **Claude Code 2.1.216**. Below it a resumed background
  24:   agent silently reverted to the default agent, and the fix that
  25:   restores "the agent's prompt and tool restrictions" landed in that
  26:   release; the probe above ran on 2.1.220, after it. So on an older
  27:   harness this seat silently reverted to the default agent - losing the
  28:   model pin, this system prompt, and the read-only tool restriction
  29:   together, which is every control the lane relies on at once. The
  30:   driver checks `claude --version` against the floor before dispatching
  31:   this seat; below it, the Fable lane is unavailable rather than
  32:   degraded, because a silently unpinned fully-tooled agent is not a
  33:   weaker reviewer, it is a different one.
  34: - From round 2 on, state position changes: accepted / refuted (with
  35:   evidence) / struck (no citation). End every round with a verdict per
  36:   claim - PASS / FIX (specific) / ESCALATE - and one verdict on the
  37:   subject as a whole.
  38: - Your terminal verdict must cite the subject revision from the brief
  39:   it verdicts on; if an amendment changed the subject, verdict the new
  40:   revision or say you have not seen it.
  41: 
  42: ## Blind protocol
  43: 
  44: Findings from other lanes reach you anonymized, with their evidence,
  45: relayed by the driver. Treat them as claims to verify against files you
  46: read - never as authority, never attributable. Do not address other
  47: reviewers; address the claims.
  48: 
  49: ## Rules
  50: 
  51: - Read-only by tool grant: no Bash, no Edit, no Write.
  52: - Cite file:line for every claim you make or contest; uncited claims
  53:   are struck, yours included.
  54: - List anything you could not verify against files you read as
  55:   UNVERIFIED - never fold unverified material into a verdict.
  56: - Report evidence and conclusions only - never transcribe your internal
  57:   deliberation.

  77: ## Containment
  78: 
   1: # Fallbacks and degraded modes
   2: 
   3: **Governing rule: no transition that reduces vendor diversity, evidence
   4: quality, or conversation continuity happens without explicit user consent.**
   5: The skill never enters degraded mode automatically. Degradation that is not
   6: consented to is a stop, not a mode.
   7: 
   8: ## The consent gate
   9: 
  10: When a failure survives bounded recovery (below), STOP and present a banner
  11: to the user — before any degraded work happens:
  12: 
  13: ```text
  14: == CROSS-VENDOR VERIFICATION UNAVAILABLE ==
  15: What failed:      <exact command + error>
  16: What degraded mode would verify:     <e.g. claims via a fresh same-vendor skeptic>
  17: What it would NOT verify:            <cross-vendor independence>
  18: Backup lane: offered when a class below qualifies it; on request otherwise — preserves cross-vendor independence; does NOT verify reviewer reasoning effort (config-only)
  19: Options: [fix codex] [run backup lane (cross-vendor preserved)] [run degraded] [abort]
  20: ```
  21: 
  22: - The user's choice is recorded in the debate record (`Authorized by:` field
  23:   — see frozen-plan-format.md).
  24: - **Unattended runs fail closed**: if no user can answer, terminate as
  25:   `BLOCKED/DEGRADED-NOT-AUTHORIZED` with a non-success status. Never wait
  26:   indefinitely, never infer consent.
  27: 
  28: ## Bounded recovery (automatic, consent-free)
  29: 
  30: One retry with the SAME model, sandbox, effort, and session parameters is
  31: the DEFAULT recovery for any failure that has no named immediate-gate rule
  32: below (codex-missing, model-rejected, auth-expired, route-mismatch,
  33: quota-exhausted, and a missing-rollout resume go straight to the gate —
  34: retrying those changes nothing). The retry reduces nothing, so it needs no
  35: consent. A failed retry goes to the consent gate. This covers timeouts and
 230: ## Degraded-mode output requirements (after consent)
 231: 
 232: - The SUPERVISING SESSION emits the banner — never delegate the banner to
 233:   the skeptic subagent's output, or a formatting miss becomes a quiet path.
 234: - Every round after the transition starts with
 235:   `== DEGRADED (single-vendor) ==`.
 236: - The finish line and the frozen plan carry the structured fields
 237:   (`Verification status: DEGRADED`, `Degradation: <class>`,
 238:   `Authorized by: user at round N`) per frozen-plan-format.md.
 239: - The debate record's Participants line must name the actual participants
 240:   (e.g. `<session model> (session) / <session model> skeptic subagent
 241:   (DEGRADED - codex-missing)`), never the cross-vendor template line.
 242: - A DEGRADED-frozen plan poisons downstream PASSes — see SKILL.md mode
 243:   `diff` for the enforcement rule.
 244: 
 245: ## Reference failures (not degraded modes — hard stops)
 246: 
 247: ### Reference source missing for a port
 248: Ask the user for the path. NEVER proceed with an ungrounded debate — a
 249: debate about remembered reference behavior is two models fabricating at
 250: each other. This is a hard stop, not a degraded mode.
 251: 
 252: ## Known limits (documented, not silent)
 253: 
 254: - The review-companion hook is ADVISORY: it injects the diff-mode reminder;
 255:   it cannot block a merge. The enforcement backstop is the finish rule and
 256:   the user. It is also registered for failed review dispatches
 257:   (PostToolUseFailure), so a crashed code review still surfaces the gate.
 258: - Hook fingerprint rot is detected by the eval suite (pinned template
 259:   fixture in CI + installed-template canary locally), not at runtime.
 260: 
 261: ## Alternative transport (documented, rejected for v1)
 262: 
 263: CLIProxyAPI (the Theo/Tibo "claudex" setup) runs a SEPARATE Claude Code
 264: process pointed at a local proxy that translates to the Codex backend
 265: (same ChatGPT OAuth), with env vars like `CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`
 266: selecting the reviewer model — it does not touch the existing session's
 267: traffic.
 268: Revisit only if codex exec proves limiting (e.g. a cross-vendor implementer
 269: lane needs Claude Code's own tool harness). It does NOT bypass the
 270: subscription tier gate — same auth, same 400 on free accounts. Costs: a
 271: local proxy service to run and keep patched, a second configured process,
 272: and untested cross-vendor hook and permission semantics.

import ast,re
root=Path('.')
def norm(s): return ' '.join(s.split())
ABBR=('e.g.','i.e.','vs.','etc.','cf.')
def split(text):
    parts=[]; start=0
    for m in re.finditer(r'[.?"'!]'"\\s+(?=[A-Z])',text):
        head=text[start:m.end()].rstrip()
        if any(head.endswith(a) for a in ABBR): continue
        parts.append(head); start=m.end()
    tail=text[start:].strip()
    if tail: parts.append(tail)
    return parts

Case  : instance10
Lines : 19
Text  : - **Rotation guard.** The offset rule assumes an append-only file, and
          the kimi client does not guarantee one. Before trusting the offset,
          confirm the stream did not rotate under the call: if after the call the
          file is SMALLER than the captured offset, or absent, it was rotated or
          replaced and every byte position from the earlier measurement is
          meaningless. That is a route-attribution failure — and specifically
          **not a reason to re-read from zero**, which is the tempting wrong
          answer: the new file's opening lines may belong to any session, so
          reading it attributes nothing while looking like evidence. Observed
          2026-07-26 (kimi-cli 1.49.0, Windows): rotation ATTEMPTS fire and fail
          with `PermissionError: [WinError 32]` because the log is still open, so
          offsets have held by accident rather than by design — do not build on
          that accident. The size test is necessary, not sufficient: a rotation
          whose replacement file grew back PAST the captured offset within the
          same call would slip through. That needs the pre-rotation offset to
          have been small, which only follows an immediately preceding rotation,
          so it is not worth a second mechanism — but if rotation ever starts
          succeeding here, compare file identity (creation time) too, not just
          length.


Case  : instance11
Lines : 10
Text  :   - route-attribution failure (offset rule in backup-lane.md): nothing

            the call is the one member that IS transient — a re-dispatch with a
            freshly captured offset would produce clean evidence. It still skips
            the retry, because the round already spent is unattributable and no
            retry can make it attributable after the fact; the user decides at
            the gate whether to spend another. Stated here so the class's
            "nothing transient" premise is not read as covering a member it
            does not describe.

from pathlib import Path
import importlib.util
p=Path('evals/tools/skill_scanner.py').resolve()
spec=importlib.util.spec_from_file_location('scanner',p)
m=importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec);spec.loader.exec_module(m)
orig=m.read_text
targets={'backup-lane.md':'rotation-guard-rule','panels.md':'panel-floor-reference','fallbacks.md':'panel-lane-loss-disposition'}
def virtual_read(path):
    text=orig(path)
    if text is not None and Path(path).name in targets:
        rid=targets[Path(path).name]
        text += f'\\n<"'!-- contract:start id={rid} -->'"\\nA contract sentence.\\n<"'!-- contract:end -->'"\\n'
    return text
m.read_text=virtual_read

print('virtual marker scan findings',len(findings))
for f in findings: print(f.as_dict())

from pathlib import Path
import ast
root=Path('evals/multi-model-verify')
for p in sorted(root.glob('test_*.py')):
    t=ast.parse(p.read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    parent={c:n for n in ast.walk(t) for c in ast.iter_child_nodes(n)}
    consts=[n for n in ast.walk(t) if isinstance(n,ast.Constant) and isinstance(n.value,str)]
    def inside_assert(n):
        while n in parent:
            n=parent[n]
            if isinstance(n,ast.Assert): return True
        return False
    outside=[n for n in consts if not inside_assert(n)]
    print(p.name,'all',len(consts),'outside_assert',len(outside),'outside_over60',sum(len(n.value)>60 for n in outside))
    for n in sorted((x for x in outside if len(x.value)>60),key=lambda x:x.lineno)[:4]:

import ast,re,subprocess

cmds={
'instance-10':\"git show 4d8a121:skills/multi-model-verify/references/backup-lane.md | sed -n '/- \\\\*\\\\*Rotation guard\\\\.\\\\*\\\\*/,/- This evidence is client-side/p' | head -n -1\",

'instance-12':\"git show f9fd9b9:skills/multi-model-verify/references/panels.md | sed -n '/\\\\*\\\\*Harness floor/,/triaged 2026-07-27\\\\.)/p'\",

pin_specs={
'instance-10':('4d8a121','evals/multi-model-verify/test_backup_lane.py'),
'instance-11':('8eacc8a','evals/multi-model-verify/test_backup_lane.py'),
'instance-12':('f9fd9b9','evals/multi-model-verify/test_seat_reshuffle.py'),
}
def norm(s):return ' '.join(s.split())
def split(text):
 out=[];start=0
 for m in re.finditer(r'[.?"'!]'"\\s+(?=[A-Z])',text):
  h=text[start:m.end()].rstrip()
  if any(h.endswith(a) for a in ('e.g.','i.e.','vs.','etc.','cf.')):continue
  out.append(h);start=m.end()
 t=text[start:].strip()
 if t:out.append(t)
 return out
for stem,cmd in cmds.items():
 body=subprocess.run([bash,'-lc',cmd],text=True,encoding='utf-8',capture_output=True,check=True).stdout
 sha,path=pin_specs[stem]
 pytext=subprocess.run(['git','show',f'{sha}:{path}'],text=True,encoding='utf-8',capture_output=True,check=True).stdout
 pins={norm(n.value) for n in ast.walk(ast.parse(pytext)) if isinstance(n,ast.Constant) and isinstance(n.value,str)}
 sentences=split(norm(body));miss=[s for s in sentences if not any(s in p for p in pins)]
 target=(any('That is a route-attribution failure' in s for s in miss) if stem=='instance-10' else any('the user decides at the gate whether to spend another' in s for s in miss) if stem=='instance-11' else any('UNAVAILABLE' in s or 'Check "'`claude'"' in s for s in miss))
 print(stem,'regions=1','sentences',len(sentences),'misses',len(miss),'target_miss',target)
 for s in miss:print(' MISS',repr(s))

---
name: verification-before-completion
description: Use when about to claim work is complete, fixed, or passing, before committing or creating PRs - requires running verification commands and confirming output before making any success claims; evidence before assertions always
---

# Verification Before Completion

## Overview

**Core principle:** Evidence before claims, always.

**Violating the letter of this rule is violating the spirit of this rule.**

## The Iron Law

```
NO COMPLETION CLAIMS WITHOUT FRESH VERIFICATION EVIDENCE
```

If you haven't run the verification command in this message, you cannot claim it passes.

## The Gate Function

```
BEFORE claiming any status or expressing satisfaction:

1. IDENTIFY: What command proves this claim?
2. RUN: Execute the FULL command (fresh, complete)
3. READ: Full output, check exit code, count failures
4. VERIFY: Does output confirm the claim?
   - If NO: State actual status with evidence
   - If YES: State claim WITH evidence
5. ONLY THEN: Make the claim

Skip any step = lying, not verifying
```

## Common Failures

| Claim | Requires | Not Sufficient |
|-------|----------|----------------|
| Tests pass | Test command output: 0 failures | Previous run, "should pass" |
| Linter clean | Linter output: 0 errors | Partial check, extrapolation |
| Build succeeds | Build command: exit 0 | Linter passing, logs look good |
| Bug fixed | Test original symptom: passes | Code changed, assumed fixed |
| Regression test works | Red-green cycle verified | Test passes once |
| Agent completed | VCS diff shows changes | Agent reports "success" |
| Requirements met | Line-by-line checklist | Tests passing |

## Red Flags - STOP

- Using "should", "probably", "seems to"
- Expressing satisfaction before verification ("Great!", "Perfect!", "Done!", etc.)
- About to commit/push/PR without verification
- Trusting agent success reports
- Relying on partial verification
- Thinking "just this once"
- Tired and wanting work over
- **ANY wording implying success without having run verification**

## Rationalization Prevention

| Excuse | Reality |
|--------|---------|
| "Should work now" | RUN the verification |
| "I'm confident" | Confidence ≠ evidence |
| "Just this once" | No exceptions |
| "Linter passed" | Linter ≠ compiler |
| "Agent said success" | Verify independently |
| "I'm tired" | Exhaustion ≠ excuse |
| "Partial check is enough" | Partial proves nothing |
| "Different words so rule doesn't apply" | Spirit over letter |

## Key Patterns

**Tests:**
```
✅ [Run test command] [See: 34/34 pass] "All tests pass"
❌ "Should pass now" / "Looks correct"
```

**Regression tests (TDD Red-Green):**
```
✅ Write → Run (pass) → Revert fix → Run (MUST FAIL) → Restore → Run (pass)
❌ "I've written a regression test" (without red-green verification)
```

**Build:**
```
✅ [Run build] [See: exit 0] "Build passes"
❌ "Linter passed" (linter doesn't check compilation)
```

**Requirements:**
```
✅ Re-read plan → Create checklist → Verify each → Report gaps or completion
❌ "Tests pass, phase complete"
```

**Agent delegation:**
```
✅ Agent reports success → Check VCS diff → Verify changes → Report actual state
❌ Trust agent report
```

## When To Apply

**ALWAYS before:**
- ANY variation of success/completion claims
- ANY expression of satisfaction
- ANY positive statement about work state
- Committing, PR creation, task completion
- Moving to next task
- Delegating to agents

**Rule applies to:**
- Exact phrases
- Paraphrases and synonyms
- Implications of success
- ANY communication suggesting completion/correctness

1. **Critical — AST collection does not distinguish pins from arbitrary strings.** The proposed collector admits every `ast.Constant[str]`, and coverage accepts any containing constant; it never verifies that the string participates in an assertion against a document ([docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-contract-coverage.md:393](C:/Users/Brandon/Documents/parallax/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-contract-coverage.md:393), [docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-contract-coverage.md:409](C:/Users/Brandon/Documents/parallax/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-contract-coverage.md:409)). A fresh simulation made the first docstring sentence “covered” despite no assertion locking it. Excluding only the checker’s test module does not close this hole ([docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-contract-coverage.md:615](C:/Users/Brandon/Documents/parallax/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-27-contract-coverage.md:615)).

   **Required fix:** define an AST pin as a string participating in a recognized assertion shape against document content, or introduce an explicit syntactic pin marker. Add a negative test where an unused constant or docstring contains the contract sentence and coverage must still fail.
